TM 11-5820-401-10-2

               WARNING

SERIOUS INJURY OR EVEN DEATH CAN HAPPEN IF THE FOLLOWING
ARE NOT CAREFULLY OBSERVED WHEN INSTALLING AND USING THE
ANTENNAS USED WITH YOUR RADIO SETS.

                              1. ARE THERE ANY POWERLINES IN YOUR AREA OF
                                     OPERATION?

2. HOW HIGH ARE THESE POWERLINES?

3. HOW TALL ARE THE POLES OR TOWERS CARRY-
      ING POWERLINES?

           MOBILE OPERATION WITH WHIP ANTENNAS
DO NOT STOP YOUR TRACKED VEHICLE UNDER POWERLINES.

@ IF POSSIBLE, TRY TO MAINTAIN  MOBILE  COMMUNICATIONS  WITH     YOUR
      ANTENNA(S) TIED DOWN.

@ MAKE SURE AN ANTENNA TIP CAP IS SECURELY TAPED ON THE END OF ANY WHIP
      ANTENNA HAVING TWO OR THREE ANTENNA ELEMENTS.

@ DONOT LEAN AGAINST OR TOUCH ANY ANTENNA WHILE THE TRANSMITTER IS ON.

@ DURING CROSS-COUNTRY OPERATION, DO NOT ALLOW ANYONE TO STICK AN
      ARM, LEG OR WEAPON OVER THE SIDES OF THE TANK. IF YOUR ANTENNA AC-
      CIDENTALLY TOUCHES A POWERLINE AND A LEG, ARM OR WEAPON CONTACTS A
      DAMP BUSH OR THE GROUND, A SERIOUS OR FATAL ACCIDENT CAN HAPPEN.

@ IF YOU ARE NOT SURE THAT AN ANTENNA ON YOUR TANK WILL CLEAR A
      POWERLINE, STOP BEFORE YOU GET CLOSE TO THE POWERLINE AND EITHER
      CAREFULLY TIE DOWN THE ANTENNA OR REMOVE ANTENNA SECTIONS TO MAKE
      SURE THAT YOU CAN SAFELY DRIVE UNDER THE POWERLINE.

@ ELECTROMAGNETIC RADIATION. DO NOT STAND CLOSER THAN 20 INCHES (.5
       METERS) TO ANTENNA AS-1729/VRC WHEN THE UNIT IS TRANSMITTING. HIGH
       LEVELS OF ELECTROMAGNETIC RADIATION EXIST IN THIS AREA.

@ VEHICLES WITH AT-912 OR AS-1729 MOUNTS MORE THAN 6’ ABOVE GROUND CAN
      STRIKE RAILROAD CROSSING POWER LINES IF THE WHIP ANTENNA COMES LOOSE
      FROM ITS TIE-DOWN DURING ROAD OR RAIL MOVEMENT. A SERIOUS OR FATAL

      ACCIDENT COULD HAPPEN.

Secure antenna tie-down

for road or rail movement               OR

depending on tie-down

clamp supplied.

WITH THE ANTENNA TIED DOWN IN THE SECURE POSITION DAMAGE TO THE WHIP
CAN OCCUR IF THE WHIP CONTACTS ANY FIXED OBJECT SUCH AS OVERHEAD
BRIDGES, ETC. THE POSSIBILITY OF SERIOUS OR FATAL: INJURY EXISTS TO OUT-
SIDE PERSONNEL FROM FLYING ANTENNA PARTS.

                                           WARNING  TRANSMITTER  YOUR-

NEVER ATTEMPT TO CARRY A RADIO RECEIVER
SELF. SERIOUS INJURY COULD RESULT.

                                                           TM 11-5820-401-10-2

            FIXED OPERATION WITH LONG RANGE ANTENNAS

                     WARNING

             TELESCOPING  EXTENDED RANGE
            ANTENNA MAST        ANTENNA

 NEVER ERECT THESE LONG RANGE ANTENNAS DIRECTLY UNDER POWERLINES.

If you must erect these long range antennas near powerlines, powerline
poles or towers, or buildings with overhead powerline connections, never
put the antenna closer than two times the antenna height from the base of
the powerline, pole, tower or buildings.

NEVER ATTEMPT TO ERECT ANY LONG RANGE ANTENNA WITHOUT A FULL TEAM

Before erecting any long range antenna, inspect all the parts making up the
antenna kit. Do not erect the antenna if any parts are missing or damaged.

Do as much of the assembly work as possible on the ground.

When erecting the antenna, allow only team personnel in the erection area.

Make sure that the area for the anchors is firm. If the ground is marshy or

sandy, get specific instructions from your crew chief or supervisor on how
to reinforce the anchors.

When selecting locations for anchors, avoid traveled areas and roads. If you
cannot avoid these areas, get specific instructions from your supervisor as
to what clearance your guy wires and ropes must have over the traveled
areas and road.

Clearly mark all guy wires and ropes with the warning flags or signs sup-

plied by your unit. In an emergency, use strips of white cloth as warning
streamers.

If you suspect that powerlines have made accidental contact with your

antenna, stop operating, rope off the antenna area, and notify your

superiors.  4

If the weather in your area can cause ice to form on your long range antenna

and its guy wires and ropes, add extra guys to support the system. Rope off
the area and post it with warning signs like ““BEWARE OF FALLING ICE.”

Do not try to erect any antenna during an electrical storm.

Keep a sharp eye on your anchors and guys. Check them daily and im-
mediately before and after bad weather.

TM 11-5820-401-10-2 —

                SAFETY STEPS TO FOLLOW IF SOMEONE IS THE VICTIM
                OF ELECTRICAL SHOCK

                 DO NOT TRY TO PULL OR GRAB THE INDIVIDUAL

                 IF POSSIBLE, TURN OFF THE ELECTRICAL POWER
                 IF YOU CANNOT TURN OFF THE ELECTRICAL POWER,
                 PULL, PUSH, OR LIFT THE PERSON TO SAFETY USING A
                 WOODEN POLE OR A ROPE OR SOME OTHER IN-
                 SULATING MATERIAL

                  SEND FOR HELP AS SOON AS POSSIBLE

                  AFTER THE INJURED PERSON IS FREE OF CONTACT WITH
                  THE SOURCE OF ELECTRICAL SHOCK, MOVE THE PERSON
                  A SHORT DISTANCE AWAY AND IMMEDIATELY START
                  ARTIFICIAL RESUSCITATION

ANY TIME RECEIVER-TRANSMITTER § (RT-246(*)/VRC OR
RT-524(*)//VRC) IS INSTALLED IN M60A2 TANKS, SPECIAL

TM 11-5820-401-10-2 ©

             HOW TO USE
            THIS MANUAL

       This technical manual tells about the typical arrangement and use of
       the AN/VRC-12 Series of Radio Sets used with intercom equipment.

       You may be required to use voice secure equipment (VINSON or
       NESTOR COMSEC EQUIPMENT) while operating. This manual also
       covers the hookup and use of the voice secure equipment.

        While using this manual, remember that your mission and type of vehi-
        cle or installation will determine the types and number of audio ac-
        cessories authorized, antennas used, cable installation and routing,
        and the physical location of the equipment.

       The appendices in the back of this manual list the various components
       that can be used with this equipment. Use the Department of Army
       Supply Bulletins to identify the various harnesses, installation kits
       and accessory kits authorized for your installation.

        As an aid while you are inventorying or transferring your equipment,
        use a copy of the proper hand receipt in HAND RECEIPT Technical
        Manual TM 11-5820-401-10-2-HR.

                                                         TM 11-5820-401-10-2

                          CHAPTER 1
                       INTRODUCTION

                       Section |. GENERAL INFORMATION

The purpose of this manual is to tell you how to operate and maintain
the AN/VRC-12 Series of Radio Sets that are used with intercom
systems.

The AN/VRC-12 Series of Radio Sets with intercom systems makeup
many different types of communication systems used in armored per-
sonnel carriers, tanks and other crew operated equipment.

Your operating procedures will be affected by the voice secure equip-
ment connected to your radios.

Another Technical Manual 11-5820-401-10-1 covers the AN/VRC-12
Series of Radio Sets used without intercom systems.

Appendix C in the back of this TM lists additional items you are re-
quired to have on hand to make your radio-intercom system work. As
an aid for property accountability, Hand Receipt Manual, TM
11-5820-401-10-2-HR is available. This TM contains preprinted DA
Forms 2620 (Hand Receipt/Annex Nos). Use copies of the proper form
when transferring or inventorying property.

                                                  NOTES

    1. The CVC HELMETS and the audio components attached to the
 helmets are NOT part of any of the above kits. CVC HELMETS and the at-
 tached audio accessories are authorized separately. The same applies to
AVC HELMETS.

    2. Use the ADDITIONAL AUTHORIZATION LIST (appx C) and Depart-
 ment of Army Supply Bulletin 11-131 to identify which kits and components
 are authorized for the radios in your tracked vehicle.

    3. Use Department of the Army Supply Bulletin 11-700 to identify the
 AUTHORIZED COMSEC INSTALLATION KIT for your tracked vehicle.

Department of Army forms and procedures used for equipment                   ©

maintenance will be those prescribed by TM 38-750, The Army Maintenance

1-6.

Refer to TM 750-244-2 for proper procedures for destruction of this equip-

The storage and shipment of these radio sets must be carefully done. Com-
plete instructions will be found in Chapter 3 in this manual.

1-8.

If your AN/VRC-12 Series of Radio Sets need improvement, let us know.
Send us an EIR. You, the user, are the only one who can tell us what you
don’t like about your equipment. Let us know why you don’t like the design.
Put it on an SF 368 (Quality Deficiency Report). Mail it to Commander, US
Army Communications-Electronics’ Command, and Fort Monmouth,
ATTN: DRSEL-ME-MP, Fort Monmouth, New Jersey 07703. We’ll send you
a reply.

                                                        TM 11-5820-401-10-2

                                  NOTES:

The following drawings show only typical hookups in tracked
vehicles.

It is not possible to show every hookup that can be made. The
 AUTHORIZED INSTALLATION HARNESS and the AUTHORIZED
 RADIO ACCESSORY KIT determine which equipment is installed ©
 in your tracked vehicle and how the equipment and accessories
 are connected.

Cable type numbers are not shown on these drawings. When
you are cabling or troubleshooting, use the cabling diagrams
 in SECTION IV of CHAPTER 2. These diagrams identify cables
 by type number.

If your tracked vehicle has an OUTSIDE CONTROL BOX
 (C-2296/VRC), the driver will need a DRIVER’S CONTROL
  BOX (C-2297/VRC).

If your tracked vehicle DOES NOT HAVE an OUTSIDE CONTROL
 BOX, the driver will have a regular CREWMEMBER’S CONTROL
 BOX.

ALL tracked vehicles will use a suppressor. Either SUPPRESSOR
 MX-7777/GRC or the MINI-SUPPRESSOR will be used.

ALL radio, intercom and COMSEC equipment in your tracked
 vehicle receive their power from the vehicle’s battery-charging
 system. The type of equipment-to-power connection is deter-
 mined by the equipment, type of vehicle and the AUTHORIZED
 INSTALLATION HARNESS.

Because of the large number of possible equipment-to-power
 connections, it is not practical to show one standard type
 connection in the following drawings. The drawings will only
 show that a connection is made to the INSTALLATION or
 VEHICLE MAIN ELECTRICAL POWER source.

       MISSION) CAPABILITY.

       AN/VRC-45.

                               CHAPTER 2
                     OPERATING INSTRUCTIONS

                                 SECTION |
       DESCRIPTION AND USE OF OPERATOR’S CONTROLS,

                    INDICATORS AND RECEPTACLES

                                      CAUTION

THIS SECTION EXPLAINS THE PURPOSE AND FUNCTION OF YOUR
CONTROLS, INDICATORS AND RECEPTACLES. EXACTLY HOW
THEY ARE USED IS EXPLAINED IN THE ‘OPERATION UNDER USUAL
CONDITIONS” SECTION. DO NOT ATTEMPT TO OPERATE YOUR
EQUIPMENT UNTIL YOU FULLY UNDERSTAND THIS SECTION.

                                       WARNING
DO NOT STAND CLOSER THAN 20 INCHES (.5 METERS) TO ANTEN-
NA AS-1729/VRC WHEN THE UNIT IS TRANSMITTING. HIGH LEVELS
OF ELECTROMAGNETIC RADIATION EXIST IN THIS AREA.

The AN/VRC-12 Radio Sets with Intercom Set AN/VIC-1 make up many dif-
ferent types of radio-intercom systems.

These systems are used in armored personnel carriers, tanks and other
crew operated equipment and weapons systems.

This makes it impossible to show ONE standard connection from the radio-
intercom equipment to the main power source.

BEFORE ATTEMPTING TO USE THE RADIO OR INTERCOM OR VOICE
SECURE EQUIPMENT, CAREFULLY CHECK WITH YOUR TEAM OR CREW
CHIEF TO EXPLAIN THE FOLLOWING TO YOU:

 © Where is the! INSTALLATION or VEHICLE MAIN POWER SWITCH

          (there may even be more than one switch)?

          Where are the fuses or circuit breakers for main power?

          What lights or gauges operate when main power is ON?

WHAT ELSE GETS POWER WHEN THE MAIN POWER SWITCH IS

ON?
                                 WARNING

SERIOUS INJURY OR DEATH MAY RESULT IF AN OPERATOR
TURNS ON A (RRIRGWERISWITGWHRIICH APPLIES POWER TO
BOTH THE ELECTRONIC EQUIPMENT AND TO A WEAPON OR
TURRET OR SOME OTHER SYSTEM.

@ What must be done before the            a 's turned ON?

Examples: Place the vehicle in PARK; engage a certain brake,
close a certain hatch; place certain controls in the neutral position,

etc.                                                       9-4

                     WHAT IS SQUELCH?                                      e

                                   When you are listening to an
                                   unsquelched fm receiver, you will
                                   hear a constant rushing and hissing
                                   sound until the distant transmitter is
                                   keyed.

                                   When the distant transmitter stops
                                   transmitting, the rushing and hissing
                                   sound returns.

With SQUELCH, your receiver is silenced during the time the transmit-
ter is not keyed. When the distant transmitter is keyed, your receiver
SQUELCH circuits are automatically disabled and you are able to hear
the message.

New fm radio sets like the AN/VRC-12 have a new squelch design to          7

automatically disable the distant receiver                   circuits.

Operating with newer sets or another AN/VRC-12 is normally done

with all receiver and transmitter           controls in the

positions.

                                   BUT

You may be asked to communicate with some older types of fm radio
sets that use an old design for disabling -eceiver cca circuits.

Operating your AN/VRC-12 radio set with older sets is done

with the receiver and transmitter ie controls in the

               positions.

A latch allows you to restrict the “SQUELCH switch move-

ment to (BB) or GMP squelch operation.

                                    NOTE:   THAT

FOR RELIABLE COMMUNICATIONS, MAKE SURE
THE DISTANT STATION AND YOU HAVE YOUR
CONTROLS IN THE SAME POSITION.

                                                                       TM 11-5820-401-10-2

2-50. OPERATOR'S INDICATORS ON THE MINI — SUPPRESSOR

In some installations, the MINI—SUPPRESSOR is used in place of
SUPPRESSOR, ELECTRICAL TRANSIENT (MX-7777(*)/VRC).

There are NO controls or adjustments on the MINI—SUPPRESSOR.

The MINI—SUPPRESSOR                     is installed in either an AUX RECEIVER  or R-T
MOUNT.

                              HEATSINK

(SOLDERED IN CIRCUIT)                                      circuits get rid of power changes
                                                           MAIN POWER source.
During operation, the MINI-SUPPRESSOR
in the power received from the VEHICLE                      happens, the fuse inside the

                                                      BUT
lf a serious overload or short circuit
MINI—SUPPRESSOR will blow.

The indicator lamp on the MINI—SUPPRESSOR will light.

This light tells you:                                      INDICATOR LAMP

                                        KEEP YOUR HANDS OFF THE FINS
                                        AND. HEAT..SHIELD.,ON. THE
                                        MINI—SUPPRESSOR. THEY GET
                                        VERY HOT AND YOU CAN GET A
                                        SERIOUS BURN FROM THEM.

---

TM 11-5820-401-10-2

                              SECTION Il
      PREVENTIVE MAINTENANCE CHECKS AND SERVICES

2-51. GENERAL

To be sure  that equipment  is always ready for your mission, you must do
scheduled    PREVENTIVE     MAINTENANCE CHECKS AND SERVICES
(PMCS).

      BEFORE OPERATION, Perform your B PMCS to be sure that your
       equipment is ready to go.
      DURING OPERATION, perform your D PMCS. This should help you

       spot small problems before they become big problems.

      AFTER OPERATION, Perform your A PMCS. This should help keep

       your equipment in top shape.
      WEEKLY AND MONTHLY PMCS are important checks you make to
       keep serious problems from suddenly happening.

   ROUTINE CHECKS like:                                                                 .
                                                                                   a
   CLEANING, DUSTING, WASHING, CHECKING FOR FRAYED CABLES,
   STOWING ITEMS NOT IN USE, COVERING UNUSED RECEPTACLES AND
   CHECKING FOR LOOSE NUTS AND BOLTS are not listed as PMCS checks.
   They are things that you should do anytime you see they must be done.

   If you find a routine check like one of those above is listed in your PMCS, it
   was listed because other operators reported problems with this item.

  WHEN YOU ARE DOING ANY PMCS OR ROUTINE CHECKS, KEEP IN MIND
_ THE WARNINGS AND CAUTIONS.

                                                 NOTE

       The PROCEDURES column in your PMCS Chart instructs you to
       “CHECK AND HAVE REPAIRED OR ADJUSTED AS NECESSARY’”’.
       Carefully follow these instructions, and remember, YOU are NOT
       authorized to open up any radio to make adjustments.

2-44

             TM 11-5820-401-10-2

@)                                                     NOTE
:    If your equipment must be in operation all the time, check and service
     those items that can be checked and serviced without disturbing operation.
5    Make the complete checks and services when the equipment can be shut
     down.

                                                         NOTE
     Use the ITEM NO. column in your PMCS table to get the numbers for the

    SPETEL RD eae °° DA Form 2404 (Equipment Inspection and

     Maintenance Worksheet) when you fill out the form.

    ment System (TAMMS) and the special instructions from your commander.

    CAUTION

    REMEMBER: Call C-E ORG MAINTENANCE if you find loose nuts and
    bolts or any defects in your radios, antennas or control boxes.

)   NOTE

    The PMCS and the criteria for READINESS REPORTING       replace Equipment
    Serviceability Criteria (ESC). Do your PMCS carefully.

                                           TM 11-5820-401-10-2

               SECTION Ill

a INSTALLATION, ASSEMBLY AND DISASSEMBLY
2-53. GENERAL  OF MAJOR ITEMS

               SE  LES
                   CAUTION

               Eas See Ae Be MR Oecre ESS

POWER OFF - When installing or

               removing any radio

                                           MAIN PWR

BREAKER-RESET                              .... THEN THE AM-1780
           R-T OFF FIRST....

                                           WARNING

THE RECEIVER-TRANSMITTERS ARE COMPACT AND HEAVY (OVER 50
POUNDS). BE CAREFUL WHEN HANDLING THEM IN ORDER TO PRO-
TECT PERSONNEL FROM SERIOUS INJURY AND THE EQUIPMENT FROM
DAMAGE. USE TWO PERSONS TO CARRY THE R-T.

                                   CAUTION

When pulling any R-T from an R-T MOUNT, carefully grasp both
guards (or handles) to pull the unit from the radio mount (straight
out) and continue to use both hands to lift and carry the unit.
NEVER CARRY OR LIFT THE RECEIVER-TRANSMITTER BY
ONLY ONE OF THE GUARDS (OR HANDLES).

                                  CAUTION

Do not lay the R-T on its back. This may damage the blower
assembly. Lay it on one side.

                                                                     2-67


---

TM 11-5820-401-10-2

A Before placing the radio on the mount, clean the mount.
B Uncover the receptacle on the mount.
C Make sure the chain for the receptacle cover

        is not between the guide pin and receptacle.

                                Ee

                      CAUTION:

   DOUBLE CHECK THE CABLE CONNECTIONS C-E
   ORG MAINT MADE TO THE RECEPTACLES ON
   THE MOUNT.

D Make sure the pins on the
        back of the radio are straight.

E Lift the radio onto the mount and carefully push it straight back to seat
        the radio pins into the mount receptacle.

F Tighten the mount clamp(s) to lock the radio on the mount.
        Install safety wire to keep clamps from loosening.

G Connect cable assemblies to the radio and mount as shown on the
        cabling diagram (see Section IV CABLING).

A Disconnect all cables connected to the radio’s front panel.
B Remove safety wire and loosen clamps.
CG Carefully remove radio from its mount (pull it straight out).
D Cover or cap all receptacles.

     on the antenna elements.

CAUTION

B Screw the two antenna elements together.
      If they are loose, wrap a few turns of electrical insulation
      tape around the joint (after putting the elements together).

C Screw the bottom section onto the top of the spring section
      of the mount.

---

TM 11-5820-401-10-2

                                           P

                                           NOTE:

          These procedures apply to all whip antennas with 2 or 3 antenna
           elements.

                                                    WARNING

         WITH THE ANTENNA TIED DOWN IN THE SECURE POSITION DAMAGE TO THE WHIP
          CAN OCCUR IF THE WHIP CONTACTS ANY FIXED OBJECT SUCH AS OVERHEAD
          BRIDGES, ETC. THE POSSIBILITY OF SERIOUS OR FATAL INJURY EXISTS TO OUT-
          SIDE PERSONNEL FROM FLYING ANTENNA PARTS.

[a] Slide the V-shaped clamp of the tie-down assembly to the middle
           of the top antenna element.

       PROPER
       TIE-
       DOWN CLAMP

            Pull the antenna down to approximately 45 to 60 degrees above
            the ground level and tie the tie-down rope(s) to the approved
            place.

                                              CAUTION

          Proper positioning for road movement/rail crossing to prevent
           antenna from snapping out and striking overhead power lines.

[<] Make sure the ANTENNA TIP CAP is firmly in place on the tip of

            the top antenna element. Use low temperature tape if necessary
            to tape the ANTENNA TIP CAP in place.

       TAPE

[]                                         CAUTION

 2-72                                                                                                    GSE Ts BS en

       When 2 or more antennas are being tied down, make sure the
       antennas do not cross each other. .

       Double check your antennas after tie-down to make sure they do
       not stick out beyond the sides of the vehicle and that no person
       walking by the vehicle can be hit in the face by the tied-down
       antenna tip.

       Remember, radio range is decreased when you are operating with
       your antenna tied down.

            2-60.1                                                   TM 11-5820-401-10-2
                    INSTALLING AS-1729/VRC ANTENNA
®)
                                       ANTENNA TIP CAP

When the antenna tip cap does not fit the AT-1095, use the following proce-
                      dures to attach the cap to the antenna section.

                                            ANTENNA
                                             TIP CAP

                    STEP 1

%s'' WIDE                          \        ee SLOT
FILAMENT
REINFORCED                              eg  TAPE BELOW
PRESSURE
SENSITIVE                                      TIP CAP
TAPE
(APPX C. ITEM 4)

                    STEP 1. Apply 6-7 layers of 1/2” wide filament-
                    reinforced pressure sensitive tape approximately
                    2” from the end of AT-1095 top section. Push Anten-
                    na Tip cap on AT-1095 as far as possible so that the
                    Slotted skirt of the Antenna Tip Assembly rests in
                    contact with the tape.

                    STEP 2                   FILAMENT
                            START           REINFORCED
                                             SENSITIVE

                                                TAPE

                                                SLOT
                                           START TAPE
                                          AT THE SLOT

                    STEP 2. Start tape at the slot. Wrap across skirt slot
                    to conform with the shape of the tip cap base so
                    that the tape goes over the cap top and returns to
                    the slotted end. Cut tape and make new start at
                    next slot, repeating the procedure in a crossing
                    pattern until all slots have been reinforced and the
                    antenna tip cap is completely covered.

                                                                            2-721


---

TM 11-5820-401-10-2

2-60. 1  INSTALLING AS-1729/VRC ANTENNA - Continued

         STEP 3

                     3/4” WIDE ELECTRICAL
                        INSULATING TAPE
                          (APPX C, ITEM 5)

         STEP 3. Start application of 3/4’ wide tape on the
         AT-1095 shaft just below the starting level of the
         filament reinforced tape and, following the tip con-
         tour while lightly stretching the electrical tape,
         wrap over the top and back to the starting position.
         Repeat until entire tip assemby is completely
         covered and the joint below the cap is neatly
         tapered to the shaft of the AT-1095.

         STEP 4

                                                                       FINISHED PRODUCT

         STEP 4. The filament reinforced tape acts to secure
         the two shells of the tip assembly together, to rein-
         force the plastic slotted skirt, to cushion against im-
         pact, and to secure the tip assembly to the AT-1095.
         The electrical insulating tape seals and protects the
         filament reinforced tape from the effects of
         sunlight and moisture. Replace tape when ex
         cessively scuffed or weathered.

2-72.2

@

                     A Remove the tie-down assembly (if used).

                     B Remove the safety wire
                           (if used) from the
                            bottom antenna element.

                                             BUT

                            Leave the (Batety wire GEER

                                       attached to the top
                                     of the spring section
                                      for future use.

@  C Unscrew the antenna element(s).

   D Store the removed antenna element(s) and tie-down(s).

   E Cover or cap all receptacles.

TM 11-5820-401-10-2

   2-62.

   A These items are normally permanently installed in the proper location.
        If it is necessary to connect cables, carefully follow the cabling draw-
        ings in SECTION IV, CABLING.

   B When installing cables, make sure that all nuts, bolts, screws and
        lock washers holding the equipment and cables in place are not loose
        or missing. Report any problems to C-E ORG MAINT.

   C When removing cables, carefully note their position and angle of con-
        nection so you can make the same connection when you re-cable the
        equipment.

   D To properly control your radio and intercom equipment with the audio
        accessories connected to the control boxes, the “‘links’” in all R-T
         MOUNTS and AUX RECEIVER MOUNTS must be connected in a cer-
        tain way. Remember, this is a job for C-E ORG MAINT.

                                                         BUT

        If C-E ORG MAINT is not handy to check your link settings, YOU can
        do a simple tests as follows:

         1 Turn the MAIN PWR
               switch on the
              MAIN JUNCTION
              BOX-AMPLIFIER

        2 Turn the VEHICLE/INSTALLATION MAIN ELECTRICAL POWER

          switch to ON and the (Gi switch on every radio to [GMM
      3 Next, one at atime, set the MG@WUERI switch on each AUX

            RECEIVER and each R-T to (BM (do the “A” R-T last).

       4  If any AUX RECEIVER or any R-T comes ON (dial light lights up),
          your links are set wrong. NOTIFY YOUR SUPERVISOR.

             range communications.

             your range.

 2-102.

@ AUX RECEIVER FAILS:

            If you have a PUSHBUTTON R-T (RT-246(*)/VRe), try using a push-
            button channel to cover the AUX RECEIVER frequency.

@ R-T ANTENNA BROKEN OR INOPERATIVE:

              If vehicle is not to be moved, use a long wire antenna.
             You may use the AUX receiver antenna, but this may reduce
              distance.

                                         NOTE

If you are not moving and you have a broken or unusable whip antenna,
a piece of field wire or other insulated wire can be used to make up a
temporary antenna.

Suggestion: Ask      your supervisor  to get you a copy of Training Cir-
                     (KNOW HOW        TO REPAIR AND FABRICATE
cular, TC 11-5
ANTENNAS).

@® CONTROL BOXES NOT WORKING

              Maintain radio communications by connecting the audio
             accessories directly to the front panel of the radio (see pg. 1-43).

@ TELEPHONE CONNECTED TO OUTSIDE ROMIRODNOBRONOMRMOING

              Reconnect the telephone to the SNE terminals on the MAIN
             JUNCTION BOX-AMPLIFIER.

@ RADIO RELAY OPERATION (USING THE MAIN JUNCTION BOX-AMPLIFIER)
      NOT WORKING:

              Ask C-E ORG MAINT to try connecting the RADIO RELAY BOX to
             the R-T MOUNTS.

@ HEAT PROBLEMS-THE R-T BLOWER KEEPS RUNNING WHEN THE R-T IS NOT
      KEYED AND THE R-T CASE IS WARMER THAN NORMAL

              Soak a towel or T-shirt in clear water. Wring it out as much as
               possible. Carefully place it on top of the R-T. Make sure it does
              not cut off the airflow behind the R-T.

                                                          CAUTIO
        If possible, use this emergency procedure only if your R-T has been
       checked out during the past 3 months by C-E ORG MAINTENANCE for
        proper R-T output and proper matching of the R-T to its antenna.

    Another way to help reduce the heat generated by the R-T is to operate the |
    R-T with the R-T input voltage at the lower limits (24 to 28 volts dc) - the
     closer to 24 volts, the better.

                           TM 11-5820-401-10-2

Recognition and identification of jamming.
                                                                                                                        /

                                                                                                                     rat
 @ A common jamming procedure is the transmission of a strong or an-

      noying signal on your frequency, making it difficult or impossible for
      you to maintain communications.

 @ Sometimes this signal may be from a friendly station.

 @® Unusual noise or interference may be from a local source or your
      receiver may not be working right.

Check out your receiver.

@ Disconnect the antenna.

@ Short out your antenna receptacle to the radio chassis.

ANT                           = COPPER WIRE

   |                       a, \

                                                                     BARE METAL SURFACE
                                                                     ON RADIO OR MOUNT

                                               AND

If the noise or interference continues, you have a bad receiver.

                                      WARNING
     WHEN YOU KNOW THAT YOU ARE BEING JAMM-
     ED, NOTIFY YOUR SUPERIOR AS SOON AS
     POSSIBLE.

                                                           2-121


---

                    TM 11-5820-401-10-2

 & Try to get rid of the effects of the jamming signal by repositioning your
        vehicle.

@ Try to use a nearby obstruction as a screen. Place your vehicle so that
        the obstruction acts as a screen between you and the possible in-
        terfering transmitter site.

@® You may get rid of the jamming by pointing your vehicle towards the
        distant station you are communicating with. Try several vehicle posi-
        tions.

© Change the setting of your VORUMEN control. This may enable you to
        raise the level of the desired signal enough to be distinguished from
        the jamming signal.

@ Ask the station you are communicating with to switch to BIGAl power
if itis on  power.

 @® If you cannot maintain communications after trying all possible pro-
         cedures, get permission to change to another frequency.

                                           WARNING
       MAKE SURE ANY ANTIJAMMING ACTION YOU TAKE
       DOES NOT MAKE YOU AN EASY TARGET FOR THE
       ENEMY. BE CAREFUL.

     TROUBLESHOOTING A RADIO SYSTEM WITH ONE R-T AND ONE AUX
     RECEIVER (AN/VRC-12 OR AN/VRC-47)

     R-T IS OK. AUX RECEIVER DIAL LAMP DOES NOT LIGHT. AUX RECEIVER IS
     DEAD.

     Step 1.  Check to see   if the AUX  receiver is firmly connected  to its
               mount.                       CAUTION:

              MAKE SURE NO POWER IS APPLIED TO THE RADIO

              Loosen the AUX receiver, pull it back, make sure the guide
              pins line up and then push the AUX receiver firmly into place
              and tighten the mount to AUX receiver clamps.

          Step 2. Check to see if the AUX receiver circuit breakers have been
                           tripped.

                      Set the AUX receiver “POWER switch to OFF and then back

            to ROENSET.

     74] R-T 1S OK. AUX RECEIVER DIAL LIGHT GLOWS. NO SOUND IS HEARD ON

               THE AUX RECEIVER.

     Step 1. Check to a defective AUX receiver front panel audio receptacle.

                   Connect your known good audio accessory to the other AUX
                   receiver front panel audio receptacle.

        R-T IS OK. AUX RECEIVER DIAL LIGHT GLOWS AND RUSHING SOUND IS
         HEARD. REPLY FROM DISTANT STATION CANNOT BE HEARD ON THE AUX
         RECEIVER.

     Step 1.  Check to see that the AUX receiver tuning controls are ac-
               curately tuning to the frequency indicated by the dial numbers.

              If possible, attempt to receiver a signal from another transmit-
              ter operating on a different frequency and then retune the AUX
              receiver to the assigned frequency.

     Step 2.  Check for best AUX receiver antenna orientation.

              If the AUX receiver antenna is tied down, untie it and reposi-
              tion your vehicle in several locations and positions. Follow
              troubleshooting instruction 10.

     AUX RECEIVER IS OK. R-T DOES NOT OPERATE PROPERLY OR IS COM-

     PLETELY DEAD.

     Step 1. Troubleshoot the R-T.

              Follow troubleshooting instructions 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13.

TROUBLE

            Test or Inspection

TROUBLESHOOTING A RADIO SYSTEM WITH ONE R-T AND TWO AUX
RECEIVERS AN/VRC-44 OR AN/VRC-48)

R-T IS INOPERATIVE. BOTH AUX RECEIVERS WORK.

Step 1.  Troubleshoot the R-T.

           Follow troubleshooting  instructions  9, 10, 11, 12, and
         | 13.

R-T IS OK BUT BOTH AUX RECEIVERS ARE INOPERATIVE.
Step 1. Troubleshoot each AUX receiver.

         | Follow troubleshooting instructions 14, 15 and 16.

ONE AUX RECEIVER IS INOPERATIVE. THE OTHER AUX RECEIVER AND
THE R-T ARE OK.

Step 1. Verify that the mount for the inoperative receiver is OK.

         Remove the good AUX receiver from its mount and replace i
           ith the AUX receiver you are trouble-shooting.

            Step 2. Troubleshoot the AUX receiver.
                           Follow troubleshooting instructions 14, 15 and 16.

TROUBLESHOOTING A RADIO SYSTEM WITH TWO R-T’S (AN/VRC-45 OR
AN/VRC-49)

          ONE R-T IS INOPERATIVE, THE OTHER R-T IS OK.

           Step 1. Verify that the mount for the inoperative R-T is OK.
                           Remove the good R-T from its mount and substitute the
                           R-T you are troubleshooting.

           Step 2. Troubleshoot the R-T.
                          Follow troubleshooting instructions 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13..,

[2] BOTH R-T’S ARE INOPERATIVE.

           Step 1. Troubleshoot each R-T.
                           Follow troubleshooting instructions 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13..

                                     APPENDIX B
                          COMPONENTS OF END ITEM LIST

4g                        Section |. INTRODUCTION
              B-1. Scope

     This appendix lists integral components of and basic issue items for the
     Radio Sets AN/VRC-12 and AN/VRC-43 through AN/VRC-49 to help you in-
     ventory items required for safe and efficient operation.

     B-2. General

     This Components of End Item List is divided into the following sections:

     a. Section Il. Integral Components of the End Item.

     These items, when assembled, comprise the Radio Sets AN/VRC-12 and
     AN/VRC-43 through AN/VRC-49 and must accompany them whenever they
     are transferred or turned in. The illustration will help you identify these

     items.
        b. Section Ill. Basic Issue Items. Not applicable.

     B-3. Explanation of Columns

     a. Illustration. This column is divided as follows:

ra.  (1) Figure number. Indicates the figure number of the illustration on

y    which the item is shown.

          (2) Item number. The number used to identify item called out  in the
                   illustration.                                        assign-

        b. National Stock Number. Indicates the National stock number
     ed to the item and which will be used for requisitioning.

        c. . Description. Indicates the Federal item name and, if required a
     minimum description to identify the item. The part number indicates the
     primary number used by the manufacturer, which controls the design and
     characteristics of the item by means of its engineering drawings, specifica-
     tions, standards, and inspection requirements to identify an item or range
     of items. Following the part number, the Federal Supply Code for Manufac-
     turers (FSCM) is shown in parentheses.

        d. Location. The physical location of each item listed is given in this

     column. The lists are designed to inventory all items in one area of the ma-

     jor item before moving on to an adjacent area.

     e. Usable on Code. Not applicable.

        f. Quantity Required (Qty Reqd). This column lists the quantity of each
     item required for a complete major item.

        g- Quantity. This column is left blank for use during an inventory. Under
     the Revd column, list the quantity you actually receive on your major item.
     The Date columns are for your use when you inventory the major item.

                                APPENDIX C
                   ADDITIONAL AUTHORIZATION LIST

                              Section |. INTRODUCTION

C-1. Scope
This appendix lists additional items you are authorized for the support of
Radio Sets AN/VRC-12 and AN/VRC-43 through AN/VRC-49.

C-2. General
This list identifies items that do not have to accompany the Radio Sets
AN/VRC-12 and AN/VRC-43 through AN/VRC-49 and that do not have to be
turned in with it. These items are all authorized to you by CTGA, MTOE,
TDA, or JTA.

C-3. Explanation of listing.
National stock numbers, description, and quantities are provided to help
you identify and request the additional items you require to support this
equipment. The items are listed in alphabetical sequence by item name
under the type document (i.e., CTA, MTOE, TDA, or JTA) which authorize
the item(s) to you.

                                                                TM 11-5820-401-10-2

                                 APPENDIX D
              EXPENDABLE SUPPLIES AND MATERIALS LIST

                                Section |. INTRODUCTION

D-1. Scope

This appendix lists expendable supplies and materials you will need to
operate and maintain the Radio Sets AN/VRC-12 and AN/VRC-43 through
AN/VRC-49. These items are authorized to you by CTA 50-970, Expendable
Items (Except Medical, Class V, Repair Parts, and Heraldic Items).

D-2. Explanation of Columns

   a. Column 1—Item Number. This number is assigned to the entry in the
listing and is referenced in the narrative instructions to identify the material
(e.g., ‘Use cleaning compound, item 5, App. D’’).

   b. Column 2—Level. This column identifies the lowest level of
maintenance that requires the listed item.

       C—Operator/Crew

       O—Organizational Maintenance

       F—Direct Support Maintenance

       H—General Support Maintenance

   c. Column 3—National Stock Number. This is the National stock
number assigned to the item; use it to request or requisition the item.

   d. Column 4—Description. Indicates the Federal item name and, if re-
quired, a description to identify the item. The last line for each item in-
dicates the part number followed by the Federal Supply Code for Manufac-
turer (FSCM) in parentheses, if applicable.

   e. Column 5—Unit of Measure (U/M). Indicates the measure used in per-
forming the actual maintenance function. This measure ts expressed by a
two-character alphabetical abbreviation (e.g., ea, in, pr). If the unit of
measure differs from the unit of issue, requisition the lowest unit of issue
that will satisfy your requirements.
